### Cyberattack disrupts global news distribution platform at France’s AFP
- Category: Cyber incidents & breaches | Deal stage: Not applicable | Publisher: The Record | Published: 2026-11-27T00:00:00Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- Summary: French international news agency Agence France-Presse was hit by a cyberattack Thursday, forcing it to take offline one of its main platforms for distributing news content to media organizations worldwide.
- Business background / implications: French international news agency Agence France-Presse was hit by a cyberattack Thursday, forcing it to take offline one of its main platforms for distributing news content to media organizations worldwide.

French international news agency Agence France-Presse (AFP) was hit by a cyberattack Thursday, forcing it to take offline one of its main platforms for distributing news content to media organizations worldwide.

The attack targeted AFP Forum, an online platform that news outlets use to browse and download the agency’s stories, photographs and videos.

AFP confirmed the incident Thursday in an internal email to employees, which was quoted in the agency’s own reporting. The agency is investigating the attack with assistance from external cybersecurity experts.

“AFP was hit by a cyber attack overnight. The target was AFP Forum, one of the platforms our clients use to browse and download our content,” AFP management said in the email.

“As soon as the intrusion was detected, our teams moved immediately to contain the intrusion and took AFP Forum offline as a precautionary measure. AFP Forum remains unavailable until further notice.”

The agency is offering subscribers alternative ways to access its content while the platform remains offline, according to French media reports .

AFP Forum remained inaccessible as of Friday, displaying a maintenance notice in several languages. The agency has not identified who was behind the attack or provided a timeline for restoring the service.

It remains unclear whether the hackers gained access to sensitive information or whether other AFP systems were affected.

AFP is one of the world’s three major international news agencies, alongside Reuters and the Associated Press. It traces its roots to Agence Havas, founded in 1835, and took its current name in 1944. It employs more than 1,800 journalists and has 210 bureaus worldwide.

This is not the first cyberattack to disrupt the agency’s content distribution services.

In September 2024, AFP detected a cyberattack on its IT systems that disrupted some of the services used to deliver news content to subscribers. The attack has not been publicly attributed to any hacking group.

is a reporter for Recorded Future News based in Ukraine. She writes about cybersecurity startups, cyberattacks in Eastern Europe and the state of the cyberwar between Ukraine and Russia. She previously was a tech reporter for Forbes Ukraine. Her work has also been published at Sifted, The Kyiv In

### British government takes services offline following ‘cyber incident’
- Category: Company & market updates | Deal stage: Not applicable | Publisher: The Record | Published: 2026-11-27T00:00:00Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- Summary: The Department for Work and Pensions said Friday that it had experienced a cyber incident, with several services taken down as a precaution.
- Business background / implications: The Department for Work and Pensions said Friday that it had experienced a cyber incident, with several services taken down as a precaution.

Britain’s Department for Work and Pensions (DWP) said Friday that it had experienced a cyber incident, with several services taken down as a precaution.

The nature of the incident has not been disclosed. In an emailed statement, a spokesperson for the DWP said the department had “taken some DWP online services offline as a precaution following a cyber incident.”

The department is the largest in the British government by spending and administers the state pension and a range of other benefits. Millions of people in the country depend on its payments for essential bills.

“Regular benefit and pension payments are not affected and will be paid as normal,” the spokesperson added.

The department said it was “working closely with the National Cyber Security Centre and Government Cyber Coordination Centre to investigate and to bring services back online as quickly as possible to minimise any disruption.”

The incident comes days after the National Cyber Security Centre (NCSC) said it has observed “a marked rise” since September in cyberattacks assisted by artificial intelligence.

Earlier this year, the British government acknowledged its yearslong approach to its own cybersecurity was flawed and warned it will be impossible to meet a previous target of securing all government organizations from known cyber vulnerabilities and attack methods by 2030.

“To protect our critical national infrastructure, defend public institutions and maintain public confidence in essential public services, we must achieve a radical shift in approach and a step change in pace,” it said at the time, warning the public sector faced a “critically high” cyber risk.

### UK’s long-awaited National Cyber Action Plan delayed until December
- Category: Products & launches | Deal stage: Not applicable | Publisher: The Record | Published: 2026-11-26T00:00:00Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- Summary: Officials, who had planned to publish the document in mid-November, now aim to unveil the strategy for defending the wider economy against state-backed and criminal hackers on December 9.
- Business background / implications: Officials, who had planned to publish the document in mid-November, now aim to unveil the strategy for defending the wider economy against state-backed and criminal hackers on December 9.

Britain has again delayed the launch of its National Cyber Action Plan, the government’s long-awaited strategy for defending the wider economy against state-backed and criminal hackers, according to multiple people familiar with the matter.

The document, originally conceived as a national cyber strategy, was first due to be published by the end of 2025. After initial delays it was rebranded as an action plan and was close to publication in July this year before being postponed amid Keir Starmer’s resignation.

Officials, who had planned to publish the document in mid-November, now aim to unveil it on December 9 according to several sources, speaking to Recorded Future News on condition of anonymity.

The reasons for the latest delay are unclear. A government spokesperson declined to comment on the timing but said ministers remained committed to publishing the plan.

The latest delay follows an influential cross-party House of Lords committee warning that the government “appears complacent” about “potentially catastrophic risks,” including cyberattacks from Russia, China and Iran.

In a report published last week, the National Resilience Committee found Britain was dangerously underprepared for crises which could cascade across the economy, with disruption spilling over multiple sectors and disrupting the everyday lives of millions of people.

Baroness Northover, a Liberal Democrat member of the committee, told Recorded Future News that the country could not afford more uncertainty or drift. “This situation is unacceptable at a time of significant and escalating risk,” she said. “We must act urgently. The cost of inaction is enormous.”

“Further absence of an overarching National Cyber Action Plan, aimed at defending the economy against state-backed and criminal hacking, is not just frustrating, it is serious,” she said.

“The UK has got to keep pace with threats. Delay and avoidance is simply not an option and underlines the Committee’s point that the UK remains seriously exposed and underprepared for very significant events, and our vulnerability is current, active and acute on many fronts.”

Successive delays to cybersecurity legislation and policy announcements have contributed to a perception that the issue is a low political priority, despite its growing importance

- Evidence: [Original report](https://therecord.media/britain-delays-national-cyber-action-plan-again)

## Behavior-based detection engineering

Generic behavior hunts are maintained separately and are not attributed to a specific campaign without source evidence:
- [Suspicious PowerShell/process execution](../../../detection-queries/kql/threat-hunting/windows_suspicious_execution.kql)
- [Scheduled-task/service persistence](../../../detection-queries/kql/threat-hunting/windows_persistence_changes.kql)
- [Entra privileged role and credential changes](../../../detection-queries/kql/threat-hunting/entra_privilege_changes.kql)

## Collection errors

- None.

## Analyst workflow

1. Match KEV/advisories to asset inventory, versions and internet exposure.
2. Verify exploit status, affected versions and remediation against primary vendor/CERT advisories.
3. Derive behavior hunts from documented TTPs; map ATT&CK only when supported by evidence.
4. Verify candidate IOCs against original source, freshness and local telemetry.
5. Keep queries Draft until schema, syntax, positive/negative tests and tuning are recorded.

## Limitations

- Broad public-source baseline, not exhaustive global intelligence.
- ThreatFox, OTX, GreyNoise and Shadowserver are not enabled; review credentials and provider terms before integration.
- Business discovery uses a wider 7-day window; Google News results are reported leads and require company-announcement or filing verification.
- Feed failures and quiet periods do not mean absence of threats; report timestamps are IST and machine timestamps in JSON are UTC.

Raw evidence: [JSON snapshot](../../../snapshots/2026/11/27.json).
